Run03 frozen prompts (single and waves use disjoint scenes)

run03_scene01_view1 | single | zh | full tokens=1912

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的陶器工坊：靠窗的木工作台上，陶艺师正在修整一只青灰色花瓶，旁边是湿润陶土、竹刀与尚未上釉的杯子。窗外院落有一棵石榴树。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在陶器工坊的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

run03_scene01_view4 | single | en | full tokens=1987

Pottery studio: A potter trims a blue gray vase at a wooden bench beside a window. Damp clay, bamboo tools and unglazed cups sit nearby; a pomegranate tree grows in the courtyard.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this pottery studio, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

在潮间带考察的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

run03_scene02_view4 | single | en | full tokens=1983

Tidal pool survey: A marine researcher examines a shallow pool between coastal rocks at low tide. Snails, seaweed and sand remain visible through clear water; a waterproof notebook rests above the waterline.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this tidal pool survey, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this hillside tea garden, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的钟表修理室：修表师在台灯下检查拆开的机械怀表，铜制齿轮按大小摆在绒布上，镊子、放大镜和玻璃防尘罩各有位置，后方是装满零件的小抽屉。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在钟表修理室的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在旧书装订的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

run03_scene05_view4 | single | en | full tokens=1984

Bookbinding workshop: A bookbinder stitches the spine of an old volume with cream cotton thread. Wooden presses, scissors and textured cover cloth lie on the desk; a small fern sits on the windowsill.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this bookbinding workshop, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

在雨后菜市场的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Market after rain: A market seller arranges wet tomatoes and eggplants in wooden crates. Water drips from the awning, soft daylight reflects on stone paving, and a bicycle leans against a distant brick wall.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this market after rain, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的雪地观测站：气象员在木屋外检查积雪标尺，厚手套握着记录夹，风向旗与小型百叶箱位于屋侧，脚印从门口延伸到仪器附近，远山轮廓清晰。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。

采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在面包烘焙间的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Bread bakery: A baker moves risen dough onto a flour dusted wooden board. Proofing baskets and linen towels stand beside the oven, with a small bundle of wheat near the window.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this bread bakery, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

在城市屋顶花园的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Rooftop herb garden: A gardener repots herbs on a city roof. Terracotta pots, a trowel and a metal watering can occupy a low bench; climbing plants frame the skyline under afternoon clouds.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this rooftop herb garden, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this riverside painting, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的木船维修：船匠在浅滩旁打磨一条木船的船舷，刨花落在干净的木板上，绳索盘成圆圈，旧船桨靠在支架边，芦苇与缓慢水流构成背景。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在木船维修的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在天文台准备的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Observatory preparation: An observer aligns a telescope inside a domed observatory. An open tool bag rests beside curved tracks and mechanical pivots; twilight and a bright star show through the dome slit.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this observatory preparation, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

在玻璃温室的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Botanical greenhouse: A botanist studies a flowering orchid in a greenhouse. Roots show through a clear pot, a misting bottle sits on a mesh bench, and condensation beads on the glass roof.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this botanical greenhouse, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的石桥晨练：一位练太极的长者站在石桥旁的空地上，宽松衣袖随着动作自然下垂，路边有长椅和落叶，浅雾笼罩着河面，桥拱倒影保持完整。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。

采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在乐器制作的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Violin making: A luthier inspects an unfinished violin at a workbench. Wood shavings and curved templates lie nearby, clamps hang on the wall, and side window light reveals the grain.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this violin making, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。

光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。

Orchard harvest: An orchard worker places fresh pears into a cloth lined basket. Ripe fruit remains among the leaves, a folding ladder leans nearby, and dappled light crosses grass beside a low fence.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this orchard harvest, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的织布工作室：织工在木织机前整理彩色经线，梭子穿过整齐的线束，几卷植物染线放在矮凳上，墙上挂着简洁织样，窗帘轻轻垂落。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在织布工作室的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Weaving studio: A weaver arranges colored warp threads on a wooden loom. A shuttle passes between the strands, naturally dyed yarn rests on a stool, and simple woven samples hang beside a curtain.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this weaving studio, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在山村邮递的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

Village mail delivery: A mail carrier checks a canvas bag beside a bicycle with a rear rack. Stone steps lead to a wooden doorway, wildflowers grow by the wall, and a winding road links hillside homes.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this village mail delivery, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

Museum conservation: A conservator wearing thin gloves cleans the rim of a ceramic vessel. Soft brushes and trays sit on a gray protective mat, with intact objects displayed in a glass cabinet behind.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this museum conservation, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

从斜侧方拍摄工作中的港口渔网：渔民坐在码头边修补绿色渔网，浮球和线轴摆在木箱上，系泊缆绳连接船身与岸桩，远处有平静水面和低矮仓库，海鸟停在栏杆上。
请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。

在咖啡烘焙的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。

请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。

Coffee roasting room: A roaster studies coffee beans beside a compact roasting machine. Metal trays hold several roast shades, sacks and scales occupy the background, and daylight enters through a ventilation window.
Use an eye-level medium-wide view with a calm, observational mood.
Create one coherent environmental documentary photograph on an actual 1024 by 1024 square canvas. The person and the described activity are the main visual subject. Keep all people adults. Use only the principal action already described, rather than adding competing activities or a crowd. Arrange the named tools and objects on credible supporting surfaces, preserving their relationship to the task. Hands should meet handles or materials correctly, with natural anatomy and clothing folds that follow the pose. Leave sufficient space around the head, elbows and working tools so that the frame does not accidentally cut through important details.
Build a clear foreground, middle distance and background. Include only a few relevant foreground objects to guide the eye toward the activity. Preserve the complete silhouette of the central subject. Use a gentle reduction in contrast and moderate background softness to convey depth, while keeping the location recognizable. Align the perspective of the floor, work surfaces and architectural elements. Doorways, windows, shelves and supports should have plausible proportions. Avoid floating objects, duplicated tools, intersecting surfaces, impossible reflections and exaggerated wide angle distortion. Preserve the practical scale of every object relative to the person.
Use physically plausible light from a direction that can be understood from the scene. Illuminate the working hands and the most important material, with gradual transitions between light and shade. A small amount of reflected light may reveal detail in the shadows, but do not flatten the whole image. Keep bright surfaces within a natural exposure range. Any rim light should be subtle rather than a glowing outline. Make contact shadows visible beneath objects and where surfaces meet. Reflections on metal or glass must agree with the surrounding space and the direction of illumination.
Distinguish materials through texture rather than extreme contrast. Wood should retain its grain and minor wear, fabric should show its weave and believable folds, and paper should retain a soft matte surface. Where plants, stone, clay, water or food occur in the brief, preserve their particular physical character. Transparent surfaces should remain transparent, and polished objects should not resemble mirrors unless their material requires it. Avoid a uniform plastic appearance. Include restrained signs of ordinary use such as small scratches, creases, damp patches or irregular arrangements, without making the environment dirty or abandoned.
Keep a natural color palette drawn from the location and its materials. Reserve a limited accent color for a small tool or item of clothing rather than scattering bright colors throughout the composition. Skin tones should respond naturally to reflected environmental light. The image should feel carefully observed, with enough detail to inspect the activity and surrounding textures, but without halos from excessive sharpening. Do not add artificial lens flares, strong color filters, dramatic motion streaks, a collage, decorative borders or a separate inset image. Exclude readable lettering, subtitles, watermarks, brand marks and labels. The final picture should communicate a specific place, an understandable action and a consistent spatial arrangement at first glance.
Within this coffee roasting room, establish a clear visual route from the person's main action to the tool or material involved, then to the surroundings. Avoid background edges crossing the head or hands. Direct the person's gaze toward the task, with a focused but relaxed expression and a practical stance. Use ordinary working clothes suited to the environment, without theatrical costume or makeup. Place frequently used tools within reach, with spare materials farther away and a credible route through the working area. Treat this as a briefly observed moment in an ongoing activity, rather than a promotional pose directed at the camera.

请将这些元素组织成一张完整、自然的环境人物照片，使用实际1024×1024的正方形画幅，不添加边框、拼贴或画中画。人物与正在进行的活动是视觉重点，环境提供明确的地点信息，但不要让背景装饰抢占主体。保留场景中描述的工具和物品，把它们放在合理的支撑面上，避免物件漂浮、重复或相互穿透。人物只做描述中的一项主要动作，手指与工具接触位置准确，衣服褶皱随姿态变化，身体比例自然。保持所有人物为成年人，不增添围观人群。
画面需要清楚区分前景、中景和背景。前景使用少量与场景有关的材质或工具引导视线，中景保留完整主体轮廓，背景通过距离和适度虚化形成纵深；不要用过强虚化遮盖空间关系。地面与台面的透视应互相一致，门窗、支架和垂直结构保持合理方向，物品大小与人物尺度协调。让主体四周留出适当空间，避免手、工具尖端或头顶被画幅边缘意外切断。
光线应来自场景内可以解释的方向，保留明暗过渡与柔和接触阴影。主光照亮主要动作和关键物件，背光只用于分离轮廓，不制造夸张光环。金属可以有细小而克制的高光，玻璃保持透明，木材、布料、纸张与植物表面呈现各自不同的粗糙度；不要把所有物体处理成相同的塑料质感。暗部依然可辨认细节，亮部不过曝，肤色与环境反射保持自然。
采用细腻的纪实摄影观感，色彩以该场景原有的自然颜色为主，只在少数工具或衣物上保留适量强调色。画面清晰但不要出现过度锐化的白边，也不要加入浓重滤镜、人工炫光或不必要的运动拖影。允许真实使用痕迹，例如轻微磨损、折痕、湿润表面和不完全规则的排列，使环境看起来正在被使用，而不是无人接触的商品展示。不要生成可读文字、标志、字幕、水印或品牌名称。最终画面应让观察者一眼辨认场景、人物动作与空间层次。
在自然标本整理的环境中安排一条清楚的视觉阅读路径：先看到人物的主要动作，再看到与动作直接有关的物件，最后注意到远处环境。使用自然的遮挡表现物体前后关系，不要让背景轮廓恰好穿过人物头部或手部。人物的视线应落在正在处理的对象上，表情专注而放松，肩膀与手臂姿态符合动作需要。选择适合这项活动的普通实用服装，颜色低调，衣料厚薄与环境相符，不使用戏剧化服饰或舞台化妆。画面中的细节应服务于具体场景，不加入与活动无关的装饰品；让常用工具靠近手边，备用物品稍远，通行位置保持合理空间。如果存在水面或潮湿物体，反射应具有正确方向和适当清晰度；如果存在植物，叶片朝向和枝条连接应自然。将这一刻表现为真实活动中的短暂片段，而不是面向镜头刻意摆出的宣传照。
